  Orderbook Delta Trading Bot Development Proposal
  
  Project Overview
  
  Based on my review of the codebase, the Orderbook Delta Trading Bot has a solid foundation across the Rust trading engine, Node.js backend, React dashboard, and strategy components.
  
  However, before moving toward production or live trading, several critical areas need to be addressed, particularly the Binance Futures integration, real time orderbook processing, execution reliability, risk management, and backtesting accuracy.
  
  I propose a 4 to 6 week development engagement to address these areas, stabilize the platform, and prepare it for controlled paper trading and subsequent live testing.
  
  Scope of Work
  
  Phase 1: Trading Engine & Critical Infrastructure
  
  Estimated timeline: 1.5 to 2 weeks
  
  * Migrate and align the trading engine with Binance Futures APIs
  * Review Long and Short order execution
  * Implement Binance WebSocket based real time orderbook/depth processing
  * Optimize orderbook delta calculation and processing
  * Review API authentication and connection handling
  * Implement order state management
  * Prevent duplicate orders and conflicting positions
  * Add robust retry and API failure handling
  * Review leverage and position configuration
  * Implement basic emergency position closure mechanisms
  
  Phase 2: Strategy & Risk Management
  
  Estimated timeline: 1 to 1.5 weeks
  
  * Review existing orderbook delta and Bollinger Band strategy
  * Improve signal confirmation logic
  * Add momentum and volatility filters
  * Add spread and volume validation
  * Add position state checks
  * Review TP/SL implementation
  * Implement position sizing controls
  * Add maximum daily loss protection
  * Add leverage and exposure limits
  * Account for trading fees, slippage, and funding costs
  * Improve execution safeguards
  
  Phase 3: Backtesting & Validation
  
  Estimated timeline: 1 week
  
  * Review the existing candle based backtesting methodology
  * Align backtesting logic with the live orderbook based strategy
  * Improve historical signal reconstruction
  * Incorporate realistic fees and slippage
  * Validate TP/SL and position management
  * Generate strategy performance metrics
  * Test different market conditions and scenarios
  * Prepare the strategy for paper trading validation
  
  Phase 4: Testing, Monitoring & Dashboard
  
  Estimated timeline: 0.5 to 1.5 weeks
  
  * Fix existing Rust test compilation issues
  * Expand automated test coverage
  * Validate trading engine edge cases
  * Improve monitoring and error handling
  * Review dashboard trade and position metrics
  * Improve real time trading visibility
  * Remove or replace the obsolete FTX Python visualizer
  * Perform end to end testing
  * Prepare the system for controlled paper/live testing
  
  Estimated Timeline
  
  Total estimated duration: 4 to 6 weeks
  
  The exact timeline may vary slightly depending on the condition of the existing implementation, Binance Futures API integration requirements, backtesting data availability, and the number of issues identified during implementation.
  
  Proposed Schedule
  
  Week	Focus
  Week 1	Binance Futures migration, architecture updates and WebSocket implementation
  Week 2	Order execution, orderbook processing and risk management
  Week 3	Strategy improvements, TP/SL and execution safeguards
  Week 4	Backtesting improvements and strategy validation
  Week 5	Testing, monitoring and dashboard improvements
  Week 6	End to end QA, paper trading preparation and final stabilization
  
  Investment
  
  Estimated Project Cost: $6,000 to $7,000 USD
  
  The final project cost within this range will depend on the depth of changes required after the initial implementation and validation of the existing codebase.
  
  The engagement will cover the development, integration, testing, optimization, and stabilization work outlined above.
  
  Deliverables
  
  At completion, the project will include:
  
  * Binance Futures compatible trading engine
  * Real time WebSocket based orderbook processing
  * Improved order execution and position management
  * Comprehensive risk management controls
  * Enhanced trading signal validation
  * Improved TP/SL and execution logic
  * More realistic backtesting methodology
  * Fees, slippage and funding cost considerations
  * Fixed and expanded automated tests
  * Improved monitoring and error handling
  * Updated React dashboard trading metrics
  * Removal or replacement of the obsolete FTX visualizer
  * End to end validation and paper trading readiness
  
  Final Recommendation
  
  My primary objective will be to make the trading system reliable, testable, and risk controlled before exposing it to meaningful live capital.
  
  The highest priority areas are Binance Futures migration, real time orderbook processing, execution and risk management, and accurate strategy backtesting.
  
  Once these components are completed and validated, I recommend progressing through backtesting → paper trading → controlled live testing → production deployment rather than moving directly into full live trading.
  
  Estimated Timeline: 4 to 6 weeks
  Estimated Investment: $6,000 to $7,000 USD

--- WINDOW  2026/08/13 12:43:48  [chrome.exe] Trading Bot Update Guide - Google Chrome
----------------------------------------------------------------------------
  [LMB]
  [Clipboard]
  Trading Bot Update Guide
  Important: Always backup your current configuration and settings before performing any updates.
  1. Pre-Update Checklist
  Backup settings.json file
  Backup .env file (if using live trading)
  Stop the currently running bot instance
  Check current version in Cargo.toml
  Review recent changes in Git history if applicable
  2. Updating Rust Dependencies
  Step 1: Update Cargo.toml
  Edit Cargo.toml to update dependency versions:
  
  [dependencies]
  reqwest = { version = "0.12", features = ["json", "rustls-tls"], default-features = false }
  tokio = { version = "1.35", features = ["full"] }
  Step 2: Update dependencies
  Run the following command to update dependencies:
  
  cargo update
  3. Rebuilding the Bot
  Step 1: Clean previous build
  cargo clean
  Step 2: Build release version
  cargo build --release
  Step 3: Verify build
  Ensure target\release\orderbook-delta-bot.exe was created successfully.
  
  4. Updating Bot Code
  Modifying Source Files
  src/main.rs - Main bot logic and trading loop
  src/helpers.rs - Helper functions and utilities
  src/order_handler.rs - Binance API integration
  src/tests.rs - Test functions
  Common Code Updates
  Adjusting Bollinger Band parameters
  Modifying orderbook depth settings
  Updating API endpoints
  Adding new trading strategies
  Improving error handling
  5. Updating Configuration
  Settings File (settings.json)
  Update the following parameters as needed:
  
  market_name - Trading pair (e.g., "BTCUSDT")
  sampling_time - Orderbook sampling interval in seconds
  bb_period - Bollinger Band period
  bb_std_dev - Bollinger Band standard deviation
  orderbook_depth - Depth of orderbook to analyze
  live - Set to true for live trading
  order_size - Size of each order
  tp_percent - Take profit percentage
  sl_percent - Stop loss percentage
  6. Updating Documentation Files
  PDF Documentation
  To update this PDF:
  
  Edit create_pdf.ps1 with new content
  Run: powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File create_pdf.ps1
  The new PDF will be generated in docs\trading_bot.pdf
  VBS Script
  The VBS script in docs\open_pdf.vbs opens the PDF automatically. Update the path if you change the PDF location.
  
  7. Testing the Update
  Pre-Launch Testing
  Run the bot in paper trading mode first ("live": false)
  Monitor logs for errors or warnings
  Verify orderbook data is being received correctly
  Check that Bollinger Bands are calculating properly
  Test position entry/exit logic if possible
  Launch Test
  run_bot.bat
  8. Rollback Procedure
  If the update causes issues:
  Stop the bot immediately
  Restore backed-up settings.json
  Revert code changes using Git or restore from backup
  Rebuild with: cargo build --release
  Test the rollback before going live
  9. Version Management
  Version Tracking
  Update version in Cargo.toml
  Document changes in a CHANGELOG
  Tag releases in Git if using version control
  Keep previous builds for rollback capability
  10. Troubleshooting
  Common Issues
  Build fails: Check Rust version and dependencies
  API errors: Verify Binance API is accessible and keys are valid
  Network issues: The bot now has retry logic built-in
  Configuration errors: Validate JSON syntax in settings.json
  11. Security Considerations
  Never commit .env files with API keys
  Keep API keys secure and rotate them regularly
  Test updates in paper trading mode before live trading
  Monitor for unusual bot behavior after updates
  12. Contact and Support
  For issues or questions about updates:
  
  Check the project repository for issues
  Review logs for error messages
  Consult the main README.md for general information
  Last Updated: August 2026
  Bot Version: 0.3.1
  c
  [LMB]
